We revisit the bound on the Jacobian of the iteration by utilizing properties of pattern distributions. We begin with a probabilistic interpretation where we consider  $p_i$  as the probability of selecting the vector  $\boldsymbol{x}_i$ . Consequently, we define expectations as  $\operatorname{E}_{\boldsymbol{p}}[f(\boldsymbol{x})] = \sum_{i=1}^{N} p_i f(\boldsymbol{x}_i)$ . In this setting the matrix

$$
\boldsymbol {X} (\operatorname {d i a g} (\boldsymbol {p}) - \boldsymbol {p p} ^ {T}) \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \tag {74}
$$

is the covariance matrix of data  $\mathbf{X}$  when its vectors are selected according to the probability  $p$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {X} \left(\operatorname {d i a g} (\boldsymbol {p}) - \boldsymbol {p p} ^ {T}\right) \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} = \boldsymbol {X} \operatorname {d i a g} (\boldsymbol {p}) \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} - \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {p p} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} (75) \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} - \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} (76) \\ = \operatorname {E} _ {\boldsymbol {p}} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {T} \right] - \operatorname {E} _ {\boldsymbol {p}} [ \boldsymbol {x} ] \operatorname {E} _ {\boldsymbol {p}} [ \boldsymbol {x} ] ^ {T} = \operatorname {V a r} _ {\boldsymbol {p}} [ \boldsymbol {x} ], (77) \\ \end{array}
$$

therefore we have

$$
J = \beta \operatorname {V a r} _ {\boldsymbol {p}} [ \boldsymbol {x} ]. \tag {78}
$$

The largest eigenvalue of the covariance matrix (equal to the largest singular value) is the variance in the direction of the eigenvector associated with the largest eigenvalue.

We define:

$$
\boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} = \frac {1}{N} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \tag {79}
$$

$$
m _ {\max } = \max  _ {1 \leqslant i \leqslant N} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \| _ {2}. \tag {80}
$$

$m_{\pmb{x}}$  is the arithmetic mean (the center) of the patterns.  $m_{\mathrm{max}}$  is the maximal distance of the patterns to the center  $\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}$

The variance of the patterns is

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {V a r} _ {\boldsymbol {p}} [ \boldsymbol {x} ] = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} - \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \tag {81} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T}. \\ \end{array}
$$

The maximal distance to the center  $m_{\mathrm{max}}$  allows the derivation of a bound on the norm of the Jacobian.

Next lemma gives a condition for a global fixed point.

Lemma A3. The following bound on the norm  $\| \mathbf{J}\| _2$  of the Jacobian of the fixed point iteration  $f$  holds independent of  $\pmb{p}$  or the query  $\xi$ .

$$
\left\| \mathrm {J} \right\| _ {2} \leqslant \beta m _ {\max } ^ {2}. \tag {82}
$$

For  $\beta m_{\mathrm{max}}^2 < 1$  there exists a unique fixed point (global fixed point) of iteration  $f$  in each compact set.

Proof. In order to bound the variance we compute the vector  $\mathbf{a}$  that minimizes

$$
f (\boldsymbol {a}) = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {a} \| ^ {2} = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {a}\right) ^ {T} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {a}\right). \tag {83}
$$

The solution to

$$
\frac {\partial f (\boldsymbol {a})}{\partial \boldsymbol {a}} = 2 \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} (\boldsymbol {a} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}) = 0 \tag {84}
$$

is

$$
\boldsymbol {a} = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}. \tag {85}
$$

The Hessian of  $f$  is positive definite since

$$
\frac {\partial^ {2} f (\boldsymbol {a})}{\partial \boldsymbol {a} ^ {2}} = 2 \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {I} = 2 \boldsymbol {I} \tag {86}
$$

and  $f$  is a convex function. Hence, the mean

$$
\bar {\boldsymbol {x}} := \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \tag {87}
$$

minimizes  $\sum_{i=1}^{N} p_i \| \boldsymbol{x}_i - \boldsymbol{a} \|^2$ . Therefore, we have

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} \right\| ^ {2} \leqslant \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \right\| ^ {2} \leqslant m _ {\max } ^ {2}. \tag {88}
$$

Let us quickly recall that the spectral norm of an outer product of two vectors is the product of the Euclidean norms of the vectors:

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {a} \boldsymbol {b} ^ {T} \right\| _ {2} = \sqrt {\lambda_ {\max } (\boldsymbol {b} \boldsymbol {a} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {a} \boldsymbol {b} ^ {T})} = \| \boldsymbol {a} \| \sqrt {\lambda_ {\max } (\boldsymbol {b} \boldsymbol {b} ^ {T})} = \| \boldsymbol {a} \| \| \boldsymbol {b} \|, \tag {89}
$$

since  $\pmb{b}\pmb{b}^T$  has eigenvector  $\pmb{b} / \| \pmb{b}\|$  with eigenvalue  $\| \pmb {b}\| ^2$  and otherwise zero eigenvalues.

We now bound the variance of the patterns:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \operatorname {V a r} _ {\boldsymbol {p}} [ \boldsymbol {x} ] \right\| _ {2} \leqslant \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}}\right) ^ {T} \right\| _ {2} \tag {90} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} \| ^ {2} \leqslant \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \| ^ {2} \leqslant m _ {\max } ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

The bound of the lemma on  $\| J\| _2$  follows from Eq. (78).

For  $\| \mathrm{J} \|_2 \leqslant \beta m_{\max}^2 < 1$  we have a contraction mapping on each compact set. Banach fixed point theorem says there is a unique fixed point in the compact set.

![](images/9abda3eaa977e180957e4c5fa6ba5db08099671a9d6dea790acbfa7f60c743be.jpg)

Now let us further investigate the tightness of the bound on  $\| \mathrm{Var}_p[\pmb{x}]\|_2$  via  $\| \pmb{x}_i - \bar{\pmb{x}}\|^2$ : we consider the trace, which is the sum  $\sum_{k=1}^{d} e_k$  of the w.l.o.g. ordered nonnegative eigenvalues  $e_k$  of  $\mathrm{Var}_p[\pmb{x}]$ . The spectral norm is equal to the largest eigenvalue  $e_1$ , which is equal to the largest singular value, as we have positive semidefinite matrices. We obtain:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \operatorname {V a r} _ {\boldsymbol {p}} [ \boldsymbol {x} ] \right\| _ {2} = \operatorname {T r} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}}\right) ^ {T}\right) - \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {d} e _ {k} \tag {91} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \operatorname {T r} \left(\left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}}\right) ^ {T}\right) - \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {d} e _ {k} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} \| ^ {2} - \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {d} e _ {k}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore, the tightness of the bound depends on eigenvalues which are not the largest. Hence variations which are not along the largest variation weaken the bound.

Next we investigate the location of fixed points which existence is ensured by the global convergence stated in Theorem A2. For  $N$  patterns  $\mathbf{X} = (\pmb{x}_1, \dots, \pmb{x}_N)$ , we consider the iteration

$$
\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {\text {n e w}} = f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) = \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {p} = \boldsymbol {X} \operatorname {s o f t m a x} \left(\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {\xi}\right) \tag {92}
$$

using

$$
\boldsymbol {p} = \operatorname {s o f t m a x} \left(\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {\xi}\right). \tag {93}
$$

$\pmb{\xi}^{\mathrm{new}}$  is in the simplex of the patterns, that is,  $\pmb{\xi}^{\mathrm{new}} = \sum_{i} p_{i} \pmb{x}_{i}$  with  $\sum_{i} p_{i} = 1$  and  $0 \leqslant p_{i}$ . Hence, after one update  $\pmb{\xi}$  is in the simplex of the pattern and stays there. If the center  $\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}$  is the zero vector  $\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}} = \mathbf{0}$ , that is, the data is centered, then the mean is a fixed point of the iteration. For  $\pmb{\xi} = \pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}} = \mathbf{0}$  we have

$$
\boldsymbol {p} = 1 / N \mathbf {1} \tag {94}
$$

and

$$
\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {\text {n e w}} = 1 / N \boldsymbol {X} \mathbf {1} = \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} = \boldsymbol {\xi}. \tag {95}
$$

In particular normalization methods like batch normalization would promote the mean as a fixed point.

We consider the differences of dot products for  $\pmb{x}_i$ :  $\pmb{x}_i^T\pmb{x}_i - \pmb{x}_i^T\pmb{x}_j = \pmb{x}_i^T(\pmb{x}_i - \pmb{x}_j)$ , for fixed point  $\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}^{*}$ :  $(\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}^{*})^{T}\pmb{x}_{i} - (\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}^{*})^{T}\pmb{x}_{j} = (\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}^{*})^{T}(\pmb{x}_{i} - \pmb{x}_{j})$ , and for the center  $\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}$ :  $\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}^T\pmb{x}_i - \pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}^T\pmb{x}_j = \pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}^T(\pmb{x}_i - \pmb{x}_j)$ . Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) \right| \leqslant \| \boldsymbol {\xi} \| \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| \leqslant \| \boldsymbol {\xi} \| \left(\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \| + \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \|\right) \tag {96} \\ \leqslant 2 m _ {\max } \| \boldsymbol {\xi} \|. \\ \end{array}
$$

This inequality gives:

$$
\left| \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) \right| \leqslant 2 m _ {\max } \left(m _ {\max } + \| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \|\right), \tag {97}
$$

$$
\left| \pmb {\xi} ^ {T} (\pmb {x} _ {i} - \pmb {x} _ {j}) \right| \leqslant 2 m _ {\mathrm {m a x}} M,
$$

where we used  $\| \pmb {\xi} - \mathbf{0}\| \leqslant \| \pmb {\xi} - \pmb {m}_\pmb {x}\| +\| \pmb {m}_\pmb {x} - \pmb {0}\|$ $\| \pmb {\xi} - \pmb {m}_{\pmb{x}}\| = \| \sum_{i}p_{i}\pmb{x}_{i} - \pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}\| \leqslant$ $\sum_{i}p_{i}\| \pmb{x}_{i} - \pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}\| \leqslant m_{\max},$  and  $M = \max_{i}\| x_{i}\|$  . In particular

$$
\beta \left| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {T} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) \right| \leqslant 2 \beta m _ {\max } \| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \|, \tag {98}
$$

$$
\beta \left| \left(\boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {*}\right) ^ {T} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) \right| \leqslant 2 \beta m _ {\max } \left\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {*} \right\| \leqslant 2 \beta m _ {\max } \left(m _ {\max } + \| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \|\right), \tag {99}
$$

$$
\beta \left| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) \right| \leqslant 2 \beta m _ {\max } \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \leqslant 2 \beta m _ {\max } \left(m _ {\max } + \| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \|\right). \tag {100}
$$

Let  $i = \arg \max_{j} \pmb{\xi}^{T} \pmb{x}_{j}$ , therefore the maximal softmax component is  $i$ . For the maximal softmax component  $i$  we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} [ \operatorname {s o f t m a x} (\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {\xi}) ] _ {i} = \frac {1}{1 + \sum_ {j \neq i} \exp \left(- \beta \left(\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right)\right)} \tag {101} \\ \leqslant \frac {1}{1 + \sum_ {j \neq i} \exp (- 2 \beta m _ {\max} (m _ {\max} + \| \boldsymbol {m _ {x}} \|))} \\ = \frac {1}{1 + (N - 1) \exp (- 2 \beta m _ {\max} (m _ {\max} + \| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \|))} \\ = \frac {\exp (2 \beta m _ {\max} \left(m _ {\max} + \| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \|\right))}{\exp (2 \beta m _ {\max} \left(m _ {\max} + \| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \|\right)) + (N - 1)} \\ \leqslant 1 / N \exp (2 \beta m _ {\max } \left(m _ {\max } + \| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \|\right)). \\ \end{array}
$$

Analogously we obtain for  $i = \arg \max_{j} \mathbf{m}_{\mathbf{x}}^{T} \mathbf{x}_{j}$ , a bound on the maximal softmax component  $i$  if the center is put into the iteration:

$$
\left[ \operatorname {s o f t m a x} \left(\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}}\right) \right] _ {i} \leqslant 1 / N \exp \left(2 \beta m _ {\max } \| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \|\right). \tag {102}
$$

Analog we obtain a bound for  $i = \arg \max_{j}(\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}^{*})^{T}\pmb{x}_{j}$  on the maximal softmax component  $i$  of the fixed point:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left[ \operatorname {s o f t m a x} \left(\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {*}\right) \right] _ {i} \leqslant 1 / N \exp \left(2 \beta m _ {\max } \| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {*} \|\right) \tag {103} \\ \leqslant 1 / N \exp (2 \beta m _ {\max } \left(m _ {\max } + \| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \|\right)). \\ \end{array}
$$

The two important terms are  $m_{\mathrm{max}}$ , the variance or spread of the data and  $\| \pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}} \|$ , which tells how well the data is centered. For a contraction mapping we already required  $\beta m_{\mathrm{max}}^2 < 1$ , therefore the first term in the exponent is  $2\beta m_{\mathrm{max}}^2 < 2$ . The second term  $2\beta m_{\mathrm{max}} \| \pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}} \|$  is small if the data is centered.

- Global fixed point near the global mean: Analysis using softmax values.

If  $\pmb{\xi}^T\pmb{x}_i\approx \pmb{\xi}^T\pmb{x}_j$  for all  $i$  and  $j$ , then  $p_i\approx 1 / N$  and we have  $m = \max_{i}p_{i}(1 - p_{i}) < 1 / N$ . For  $M\leqslant 1 / \sqrt{2\beta}$  we obtain from Lemma A2:

$$
\left\| \mathrm {J} \right\| _ {2} <   1. \tag {104}
$$

The local fixed point is  $\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}^{*} \approx \pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}} = (1 / N) \sum_{i=1}^{N} \pmb{x}_{i}$  with  $p_{i} \approx 1 / N$ .

We now treat this case more formally. First we discuss conditions that ensure that the iteration is a contraction mapping. We consider the iteration Eq. (57) in the variable  $p$ :

$$
\boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}} = g (\boldsymbol {p}) = \operatorname {s o f t m a x} \left(\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {p}\right). \tag {105}
$$

The Jacobian is

$$
J (\boldsymbol {p}) = \frac {\partial g (\boldsymbol {p})}{\partial \boldsymbol {p}} = \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {X} J _ {s} \tag {106}
$$

with

$$
\mathrm {J} _ {s} \left(\boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}}\right) = \beta \left(\operatorname {d i a g} \left(\boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}}\right) - \boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}} \left(\boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}}\right) ^ {T}\right). \tag {107}
$$

The version of the mean value theorem in Lemma A32 states for  $\mathrm{J}^m = \int_0^1\mathrm{J}(\lambda \pmb {p})\mathrm{d}\lambda = \pmb {X}^T\pmb {X}\mathrm{J}_s^m$  with the symmetric matrix  $\mathrm{J}_s^m = \int_0^1\mathrm{J}_s(\lambda \pmb {p})\mathrm{d}\lambda$

$$
\boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}} = g (\boldsymbol {p}) = g (\boldsymbol {0}) + \left(\mathrm {J} ^ {m}\right) ^ {T} \boldsymbol {p} = g (\boldsymbol {0}) + \mathrm {J} _ {s} ^ {m} \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {p} = 1 / N \mathbf {1} + \mathrm {J} _ {s} ^ {m} \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {p}. \tag {108}
$$

With  $m = \max_{i}p_{i}(1 - p_{i})$ , Eq. (476) from Lemma A24 is

$$
\left\| \mathrm {J} _ {s} (\boldsymbol {p}) \right\| _ {2} = \beta \left\| \operatorname {d i a g} (\boldsymbol {p}) - \boldsymbol {p} \boldsymbol {p} ^ {T} \right\| _ {2} \leqslant 2 m \beta . \tag {109}
$$

First observe that  $\lambda p_i(1 - \lambda p_i) \leqslant p_i(1 - p_i)$  for  $p_i \leqslant 0.5$  and  $\lambda \in [0,1]$ , since  $p_i(1 - p_i) - \lambda p_i(1 - \lambda p_i) = (1 - \lambda)p_i(1 - (1 + \lambda)p_i) \geq 0$ . For  $\max_i p_i \leqslant 0.5$  this observation leads to the following bound for  $\mathrm{J}_s^m$ :

$$
\left\| \mathrm {J} _ {s} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \leqslant 2 m \beta . \tag {110}
$$

Eq. (479) in Lemma A24 states that every  $\mathrm{J}_s$  is bounded by  $1 / 2\beta$ , therefore also the mean:

$$
\left\| \mathrm {J} _ {s} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \leqslant 0. 5 \beta . \tag {111}
$$

Since  $m = \max_{i} p_{i}(1 - p_{i}) < \max_{i} p_{i} = p_{\max}$ , the previous bounds can be combined as follows:

$$
\left\| J _ {s} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \leqslant 2 \min  \{0. 2 5, p _ {\max } \} \beta . \tag {112}
$$

Consequently,

$$
\left\| J ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \leqslant N M ^ {2} 2 \min  \{0. 2 5, p _ {\max } \} \beta , \tag {113}
$$

where we used Eq. (170).  $\left\| \mathbf{X}^T\mathbf{X}\right\| _2 = \left\| \mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^T\right\| _2$  , therefore  $\left\| \mathbf{X}^T\mathbf{X}\right\| _2$  is  $N$  times the maximal second moment of the data squared.

Obviously,  $g(\pmb{p})$  is a contraction mapping in compact sets, where

$$
N M ^ {2} 2 \min  \{0. 2 5, p _ {\max } \} \beta <   1. \tag {114}
$$

S is the sphere around the origin 0 with radius one. For

$$
\boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}} = g (\boldsymbol {p}) = 1 / N \mathbf {1} + \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \boldsymbol {p}, \tag {115}
$$

we have  $\| \pmb{p}\| \leqslant \| \pmb{p}\| _1 = 1$  and  $\| \pmb{p}^{\mathrm{new}}\| \leqslant \| \pmb{p}^{\mathrm{new}}\| _1 = 1$ . Therefore,  $g$  maps points from S into S.  $g$  is a contraction mapping for

$$
\left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \leqslant N M ^ {2} 2 \min  \{0. 2 5, p _ {\max } \} \beta = c <   1. \tag {116}
$$

According to Banach fixed point theorem  $g$  has a fixed point in the sphere  $S$ .

Holder's inequality gives:

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {p} \right\| ^ {2} = \boldsymbol {p} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {p} \leqslant \left\| \boldsymbol {p} \right\| _ {1} \left\| \boldsymbol {p} \right\| _ {\infty} = \left\| \boldsymbol {p} \right\| _ {\infty} = p _ {\max } \tag {117}
$$

Alternatively:

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {p} \right\| ^ {2} = \sum_ {i} p _ {i} ^ {2} = p _ {\max } \sum_ {i} \frac {p _ {i}}{p _ {\max }} p _ {i} \leqslant p _ {\max } \sum_ {i} p _ {i} = p _ {\max }. \tag {118}
$$

Let now S be the sphere around the origin 0 with radius  $1 / \sqrt{N} +\sqrt{p_{\mathrm{max}}}$  and let  $\| \mathrm{J}^m (\pmb {p})\| _2\leqslant c < 1$  for  $\pmb {p}\in \mathbf{S}$ . The old  $\pmb{p}$  is in the sphere S  $(\pmb {p}\in \mathbf{S})$  since  $p_{\mathrm{max}} < \sqrt{p_{\mathrm{max}}}$  for  $p_{\mathrm{max}} < 1$ . We have

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}} \right\| \leqslant 1 / \sqrt {N} + \left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {p} \| \leqslant 1 / \sqrt {N} + \sqrt {p _ {\max }}. \tag {119}
$$

Therefore,  $g$  is a mapping from S into S and a contraction mapping. According to Banach fixed point theorem, a fixed point exists in S.

For the 1-norm, we use Lemma A24 and  $\| \pmb{p}\| _1 = 1$  to obtain from Eq. (115):

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}} - 1 / N \mathbf {1} \right\| _ {1} \leqslant \left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {1} \leqslant 2 \beta m \| \boldsymbol {X} \| _ {\infty} M _ {1}, \tag {120}
$$

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}} - 1 / N \mathbf {1} \right\| _ {1} \leqslant \left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {1} \leqslant 2 \beta m N M _ {\infty} M _ {1}, \tag {121}
$$

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}} - 1 / N \mathbf {1} \right\| _ {1} \leqslant \left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {1} \leqslant 2 \beta m N M ^ {2}, \tag {122}
$$

where  $m = \max_{i} p_{i}(1 - p_{i})$ ,  $M_{1} = \| \mathbf{X}\|_{1} = \max_{i}\| \pmb{x}_{i}\|_{1}$ ,  $M = \max_{i}\| \pmb{x}_{i}\|$ ,  $\| \mathbf{X}\|_{\infty} = \left\| \mathbf{X}^{T}\right\|_{1} = \max_{i}\left\| [X^{T}]_{i}\right\|_{1}$  (maximal absolute row sum norm), and  $M_{\infty} = \max_{i}\| \pmb{x}_{i}\|_{\infty}$ . Let us quickly mention some auxiliary estimates related to  $X^T X$ :

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {X} \right\| _ {1} = \max  _ {i} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {N} \left| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right| \leqslant \max  _ {i} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {N} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| _ {\infty} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| _ {1} \tag {123}
$$

$$
\leqslant M _ {\infty} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {N} M _ {1} = N M _ {\infty} M _ {1},
$$

where the first inequality is from Hölder's inequality. We used

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {X} \right\| _ {1} = \max  _ {i} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {N} \left| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right| \leqslant \max  _ {i} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {N} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| \tag {124} \\ \leqslant M \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {N} M = N M ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality is from Hölder's inequality (here the same as the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality). See proof of Lemma A24 for the 1-norm bound on  $J_{s}$ . Everything else follows from the fact that the 1-norm is sub-multiplicative as induced matrix norm.

We consider the minimal  $\| \pmb {p}\|$

$$
\min  _ {\boldsymbol {p}} \| \boldsymbol {p} \| ^ {2} \tag {125}
$$

$$
\text {s . t .} \quad \sum_ {i} p _ {i} = 1
$$

$$
\forall_ {i}: p _ {i} \geq 0.
$$

The solution to this minimization problem is  $\pmb{p} = (1 / N)\mathbf{1}$ . Therefore, we have  $1 / \sqrt{N} \leqslant \| \pmb{p} \|$  and  $1 / N \leqslant \| \pmb{p} \|^2$ . Using Eq. (119) we obtain

$$
1 / \sqrt {N} \leqslant \| \boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}} \| \leqslant 1 / \sqrt {N} + \sqrt {p _ {\max }}. \tag {126}
$$

Moreover

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}} \right\| ^ {2} = \left(\boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}}\right) ^ {T} \boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}} = 1 / N + \left(\boldsymbol {p} ^ {\text {n e w}}\right) ^ {T} \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \boldsymbol {p} \leqslant 1 / N + \left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {p} \| \tag {127} \\ \leqslant 1 / N + \left\| J ^ {m} \right\| _ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

since  $\pmb{p}^{\mathrm{new}} \in \mathrm{S}$  and  $\pmb{p} \in \mathrm{S}$ .

For the fixed point, we have

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {p} ^ {*} \right\| ^ {2} = \left(\boldsymbol {p} ^ {*}\right) ^ {T} \boldsymbol {p} ^ {*} = 1 / N + \left(\boldsymbol {p} ^ {*}\right) ^ {T} \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \boldsymbol {p} ^ {*} \leqslant 1 / N + \left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {p} ^ {*} \right\| ^ {2}, \tag {128}
$$

and hence

$$
1 / N \leqslant \| \boldsymbol {p} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \leqslant 1 / N \frac {1}{1 - \| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \| _ {2}} = 1 / N \left(1 + \frac {\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \| _ {2}}{1 - \| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \| _ {2}}\right). \tag {129}
$$

Therefore, for small  $\| \mathrm{J}^m\| _2$  we have  $\pmb {p}^{*}\approx (1 / N)\mathbf{1}$

A.1.5.3 Many Stable States: Fixed Points Near Stored Patterns. We move on to the next case, where the patterns  $\pmb{x}_i$  are well separated. In this case the iteration goes to the pattern to which the initial  $\xi$  is most similar. If the initial  $\xi$  is similar to a vector  $\pmb{x}_i$  then it will converge to  $\pmb{x}_i$  and  $\pmb{p}$  will be  $e_i$ . The main ingredients are again Banach's Theorem and estimates on the Jacobian norm.

Proof of a fixed point by Banach Fixed Point Theorem.

$\rightarrow$  Mapped Vectors Stay in a Compact Environment. We show that if  $\pmb{x}_i$  is sufficient dissimilar to other  $\pmb{x}_j$  then there is an compact environment of  $\pmb{x}_i$  (a sphere) where the fixed point iteration maps this environment into itself. The idea of the proof is to define a sphere around  $\pmb{x}_i$  for which points from the sphere are mapped by  $f$  into the sphere.

We first need following lemma which bounds the distance  $\| \pmb{x}_i - f(\pmb{\xi}) \|$ , where  $\pmb{x}_i$  is the pattern that is least separated from  $\pmb{\xi}$  but separated from other patterns.

Lemma A4. For a query  $\pmb{\xi}$  and data  $\mathbf{X} = (\pmb{x}_1, \dots, \pmb{x}_N)$ , there exists a  $\pmb{x}_i$  that is least separated from  $\pmb{\xi}$  while being separated from other  $\pmb{x}_j$  with  $j \neq i$ :

$$
i = \arg \max  _ {k} \min  _ {j, j \neq k} \left(\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {k} - \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) = \arg \max  _ {k} \left(\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {k} - \max  _ {j, j \neq k} \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) \tag {130}
$$

$$
0 \leqslant c = \max  _ {k} \min  _ {j, j \neq k} \left(\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {k} - \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) = \max  _ {k} \left(\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {k} - \max  _ {j, j \neq k} \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right). \tag {131}
$$

For  $\pmb{x}_i$ , the following holds:

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) \right\| \leqslant 2 \in M, \tag {132}
$$

where

$$
M = \max  _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \|, \tag {133}
$$

$$
\epsilon = (N - 1) \exp (- \beta c). \tag {134}
$$

Proof. For the softmax component  $i$  we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} [ \operatorname {s o f t m a x} (\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {\xi}) ] _ {i} = \frac {1}{1 + \sum_ {j \neq i} \exp \left(\beta \left(\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} - \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right)\right)} \geq \frac {1}{1 + \sum_ {j \neq i} \exp (- \beta c)} \tag {135} \\ = \frac {1}{1 + (N - 1) \exp (- \beta c)} = 1 - \frac {(N - 1) \exp (- \beta c)}{1 + (N - 1) \exp (- \beta c)} \\ \geq 1 - (N - 1) \exp (- \beta c) = 1 - \epsilon \\ \end{array}
$$

For softmax components  $k \neq i$  we have

$$
[ \operatorname {s o f t m a x} (\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {\xi}) ] _ {k} = \frac {\exp (\beta (\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {k} - \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}))}{1 + \sum_ {j \neq i} \exp (\beta (\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} - \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}))} \leqslant \exp (- \beta c) = \frac {\epsilon}{N - 1}. \tag {136}
$$

The iteration  $f$  can be written as

$$
f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) = \boldsymbol {X} \operatorname {s o f t m a x} \left(\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {\xi}\right) = \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {N} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \left[ \operatorname {s o f t m a x} \left(\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {\xi}\right) \right] _ {j}. \tag {137}
$$

We now can bound  $\| x_{i} - f(\pmb {\xi})\|$  ..

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) \right\| = \left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {N} [ \operatorname {s o f t m a x} \left(\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {\xi}\right) ] _ {j} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\| \tag {138} \\ = \left\| \left(1 - \left[ \operatorname {s o f t m a x} \left(\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {\xi}\right) \right] _ {i}\right) \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \sum_ {j = 1, j \neq i} ^ {N} \left[ \operatorname {s o f t m a x} \left(\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {\xi}\right) \right] _ {j} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\| \\ \leqslant \epsilon \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| + \frac {\epsilon}{N - 1} \sum_ {j = 1, j \neq i} ^ {N} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| \\ \leqslant \epsilon M + \frac {\epsilon}{N - 1} \sum_ {j = 1, j \neq i} ^ {N} M = 2 \epsilon M. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/703f3e84f839a6d80d51abc141602276e432d4d6c2637a48e2ec639035e9f1dc.jpg)

We define  $\Delta_{i}$ , i.e. the separation of pattern  $\pmb{x}_i$  from data  $\pmb{X} = (\pmb{x}_1, \dots, \pmb{x}_N)$  as:

$$
\Delta_ {i} = \min  _ {j, j \neq i} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) = \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \max  _ {j, j \neq i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}. \tag {139}
$$

The pattern is separated from the other data if  $0 < \Delta_{i}$ . Using the parallelogram identity,  $\Delta_{i}$  can also be expressed as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \Delta_ {i} = \min  _ {j, j \neq i} \frac {1}{2} \left(\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| ^ {2} - \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| ^ {2}\right) \tag {140} \\ = \frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2} \max  _ {j, j \neq i} \left(\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| ^ {2} - \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| ^ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

For  $\| \pmb{x}_i\| = \| \pmb{x}_j\|$  we have  $\Delta_{i} = 1 / 2\min_{j,j\neq i}\left\| \pmb{x}_{i} - \pmb{x}_{j}\right\|^{2}$ .

Next we define the sphere where we want to apply Banach fixed point theorem.

Definition 3 (Sphere  $S_i$ ). The sphere  $S_i$  is defined as

$$
\mathrm {S} _ {i} := \left\{\boldsymbol {\xi} \mid \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \leqslant \frac {1}{\beta N M} \right\}. \tag {141}
$$

Lemma A5. With  $\xi$  given, if the assumptions

A1:  $\pmb{\xi}$  is inside sphere:  $\pmb {\xi}\in \mathrm{S}_i$

A2: data point  $\pmb{x}_i$  is well separated from the other data:

$$
\Delta_ {i} \geq \frac {2}{\beta N} + \frac {1}{\beta} \ln (2 (N - 1) N \beta M ^ {2}) \tag {142}
$$

hold, then  $f(\pmb{\xi})$  is inside the sphere:  $f(\pmb{\xi}) \in \mathrm{S}_i$ . Therefore, with assumption (A2),  $f$  is a mapping from  $\mathrm{S}_i$  into  $\mathrm{S}_i$ .

Proof. We need the separation  $\tilde{\Delta}_i$  of  $\pmb{\xi}$  from the data.

$$
\tilde {\Delta} _ {i} = \min  _ {j, j \neq i} \left(\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right). \tag {143}
$$

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain for  $1 \leqslant j \leqslant N$ :

$$
\left| \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right| \leqslant \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| \leqslant \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| M. \tag {144}
$$

We have the lower bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tilde {\Delta} _ {i} \geq \min  _ {j, j \neq i} \left(\left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| M\right) - \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} + \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| M\right)\right) \tag {145} \\ = - 2 \left\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| M + \min  _ {j, j \neq i} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) = \Delta_ {i} - 2 \left\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| M \\ \geq \Delta_ {i} - \frac {2}{\beta N}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used the assumption (A1) of the lemma.

From the proof in Lemma A4 we have

$$
p _ {\max } = \left[ \operatorname {s o f t m a x} \left(\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {\xi}\right) \right] _ {i} \geq 1 - (N - 1) \exp \left(- \beta \tilde {\Delta} _ {i}\right) = 1 - \tilde {\epsilon}. \tag {146}
$$

Lemma A4 states that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) \right\| \leqslant 2 \tilde {\epsilon} M = 2 (N - 1) \exp (- \beta \tilde {\Delta} _ {i}) M \tag {147} \\ \leqslant 2 (N - 1) \exp \left(- \beta \left(\Delta_ {i} - \frac {2}{\beta N}\right)\right) M. \\ \end{array}
$$

We have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) \right\| \tag {148} \\ \leqslant 2 (N - 1) \exp \left(- \beta \left(\frac {2}{\beta N} + \frac {1}{\beta} \ln \left(2 (N - 1) N \beta M ^ {2}\right) - \frac {2}{\beta N}\right)\right) M \\ = 2 (N - 1) \exp (- \ln (2 (N - 1) N \beta M ^ {2})) M \\ = \frac {1}{N \beta M}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used assumption (A2) of the lemma. Therefore,  $f(\pmb{\xi})$  is a mapping from the sphere  $S_i$  into the sphere  $S_i$ : If  $\pmb{\xi} \in S_i$  then  $f(\pmb{\xi}) \in S_i$ .

- Contraction mapping.

For applying Banach fixed point theorem we need to show that  $f$  is contraction in the compact environment  $S_{i}$ .

Lemma A6. Assume that

A1:

$$
\Delta_ {i} \geq \frac {2}{\beta N} + \frac {1}{\beta} \ln (2 (N - 1) N \beta M ^ {2}), \tag {149}
$$

then  $f$  is a contraction mapping in  $S_i$ .

Proof. The version of the mean value theorem Lemma A32 states for  $\mathrm{J}^m = \int_0^1\mathrm{J}(\lambda \pmb {\xi} + (1 - \lambda)\pmb {x}_i)  \mathrm{d}\lambda$ :

$$
f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) = f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) + \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \left(\boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right). \tag {150}
$$

Therefore

$$
\left\| f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) - f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \right\| \leqslant \left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \|. \tag {151}
$$

We define  $\tilde{\pmb{\xi}} = \lambda \pmb {\xi} + (1 - \lambda)\pmb{x}_i$  for some  $\lambda \in [0,1]$ . From the proof in Lemma A4 we have

$$
p _ {\max } (\tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}}) = \left[ \operatorname {s o f t m a x} \left(\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}}\right) \right] _ {i} \geq 1 - (N - 1) \exp (- \beta \tilde {\Delta} _ {i}) = 1 - \tilde {\epsilon}, \tag {152}
$$

$$
\tilde {\epsilon} = (N - 1) \exp (- \beta \tilde {\Delta} _ {i}), \tag {153}
$$

$$
\tilde {\Delta} _ {i} = \min  _ {j, j \neq i} \left(\tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right). \tag {154}
$$

First we compute an upper bound on  $\tilde{\epsilon}$ . We need the separation  $\tilde{\Delta}_i$  of  $\pmb{\xi}$  from the data. Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain for  $1\leqslant j\leqslant N$ :

$$
\left| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right| \leqslant \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| \leqslant \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| M. \tag {155}
$$

We have the lower bound on  $\tilde{\Delta}_i$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tilde {\Delta} _ {i} \geq \min  _ {j, j \neq i} \left(\left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| M\right) - \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} + \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| M\right)\right) \tag {156} \\ = - 2 \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| M + \min  _ {j, j \neq i} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) = \Delta_ {i} - 2 \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| M \\ \geq \Delta_ {i} - 2 \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| M, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $\left\| \tilde{\pmb{\xi}} - \pmb{x}_i \right\| = \lambda \| \pmb{\xi} - \pmb{x}_i \| \leqslant \| \pmb{\xi} - \pmb{x}_i \|$ . From the definition of  $\tilde{\epsilon}$  in Eq. (152) we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tilde {\epsilon} = (N - 1) \exp (- \beta \tilde {\Delta} _ {i}) \tag {157} \\ \leqslant (N - 1) \exp (- \beta (\Delta_ {i} - 2 \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| M)) \\ \leqslant (N - 1) \exp \left(- \beta \left(\Delta_ {i} - \frac {2}{\beta N}\right)\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $\pmb {\xi}\in \mathrm{S}_i$  , therefore  $\| \pmb {\xi} - \pmb {x}_i\| \leqslant \frac{1}{\beta N M}.$

Next we compute an lower bound on  $\tilde{\epsilon}$ . We start with an upper on  $\tilde{\Delta}_i$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tilde {\Delta} _ {i} \leqslant \min  _ {j, j \neq i} \left(\left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} + \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| M\right) - \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} - \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| M\right)\right) \tag {158} \\ = 2 \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| M + \min  _ {j, j \neq i} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) = \Delta_ {i} + 2 \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| M \\ \leqslant \Delta_ {i} + 2 \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| M, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $\left\| \tilde{\pmb{\xi}} - \pmb{x}_i \right\| = \lambda \| \pmb{\xi} - \pmb{x}_i \| \leqslant \| \pmb{\xi} - \pmb{x}_i \|$ . From the definition of  $\tilde{\epsilon}$  in Eq. (152) we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tilde {\epsilon} = (N - 1) \exp (- \beta \tilde {\Delta} _ {i}) \tag {159} \\ \geq (N - 1) \exp (- \beta (\Delta_ {i} + 2 \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| M)) \\ \geq (N - 1) \exp \left(- \beta \left(\Delta_ {i} + \frac {2}{\beta N}\right)\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $\pmb {\xi}\in \mathrm{S}_i$  , therefore  $\| \pmb {\xi} - \pmb {x}_i\| \leqslant \frac{1}{\beta N M}$

Now we bound the Jacobian. We can assume  $\tilde{\epsilon} \leqslant 0.5$  otherwise  $(1 - \tilde{\epsilon}) \leqslant 0.5$  in the following. From the proof of Lemma A24 we know for  $p_{\max}(\tilde{\xi}) \geq 1 - \tilde{\epsilon}$ , then  $p_i(\tilde{\xi}) \leqslant \tilde{\epsilon}$  for  $p_i(\tilde{\xi}) \neq p_{\max}(\tilde{\xi})$ .

Therefore,  $p_i(\tilde{\xi})(1 - p_i(\tilde{\xi})) \leqslant m \leqslant \tilde{\epsilon}(1 - \tilde{\epsilon})$  for all  $i$ . Next we use the derived upper and lower bound on  $\tilde{\epsilon}$  in previous Eq. (61) in Lemma A2:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathrm {J} (\tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}}) \right\| _ {2} \leqslant 2 \beta N M ^ {2} \tilde {\epsilon} - 2 \tilde {\epsilon} ^ {2} \beta N M ^ {2} \tag {160} \\ \leqslant 2 \beta N M ^ {2} (N - 1) \exp \left(- \beta \left(\Delta_ {i} - \frac {2}{\beta N}\right)\right) - \\ 2 (N - 1) ^ {2} \exp \left(- 2 \beta \left(\Delta_ {i} + \frac {2}{\beta N}\right)\right) \beta N M ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

The bound Eq. (160) holds for the mean  $\mathrm{J}^m$ , too, since it averages over  $\mathrm{J}(\tilde{\xi})$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \leqslant 2 \beta N M ^ {2} (N - 1) \exp \left(- \beta \left(\Delta_ {i} - \frac {2}{\beta N}\right)\right) - \tag {161} \\ 2 (N - 1) ^ {2} \exp \left(- 2 \beta \left(\Delta_ {i} + \frac {2}{\beta N}\right)\right) \beta N M ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

The assumption of the lemma is

$$
\Delta_ {i} \geq \frac {2}{\beta N} + \frac {1}{\beta} \ln (2 (N - 1) N \beta M ^ {2}), \tag {162}
$$

This is

$$
\Delta_ {i} - \frac {2}{\beta N} \geq \frac {1}{\beta} \ln (2 (N - 1) N \beta M ^ {2}), \tag {163}
$$

Therefore, the spectral norm  $\| \mathrm{J}\| _2$  can be bounded by:

$$
\left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \leqslant 2 \beta (N - 1) \exp \left(- \beta \frac {1}{\beta} \ln (2 (N - 1) N \beta M ^ {2})\right) N M ^ {2} - \tag {164}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} 2 (N - 1) ^ {2} \exp \left(- 2 \beta \left(\Delta_ {i} + \frac {2}{\beta N}\right)\right) \beta N M ^ {2} \\ = 2 \beta (N - 1) \frac {1}{2 (N - 1) N \beta M ^ {2}} N M ^ {2} - \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} 2 (N - 1) ^ {2} \exp \left(- 2 \beta \left(\Delta_ {i} + \frac {2}{\beta N}\right)\right) \beta N M ^ {2} \\ = 1 - 2 (N - 1) ^ {2} \exp \left(- 2 \beta \left(\Delta_ {i} + \frac {2}{\beta N}\right)\right) \beta N M ^ {2} <   1. \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore,  $f$  is a contraction mapping in  $S_{i}$ .

![](images/1139ee690f1440eecc0bd4aa55032437cd23354d9528d6520955123bc01fe5f3.jpg)

- Banach Fixed Point Theorem. Now we have all ingredients to apply Banach fixed point theorem.

Lemma A7. Assume that

A1:

$$
\Delta_ {i} \geq \frac {2}{\beta N} + \frac {1}{\beta} \ln (2 (N - 1) N \beta M ^ {2}), \tag {165}
$$

then  $f$  has a fixed point in  $S_{i}$ .

Proof. We use Banach fixed point theorem: Lemma A5 says that  $f$  maps from  $S_i$  into  $S_i$ . Lemma A6 says that  $f$  is a contraction mapping in  $S_i$ .

- Contraction mapping with a fixed point.

We have shown that a fixed point exists. We want to know how fast the iteration converges to the fixed point. Let  $\boldsymbol{x}_i^*$  be the fixed point of the iteration  $f$  in the sphere  $S_i$ . Using the mean value theorem Lemma A32, we have with  $\mathrm{J}^m = \int_0^1 \mathrm{J}(\lambda \boldsymbol{\xi} + (1 - \lambda) \boldsymbol{x}_i^*) \, \mathrm{d}\lambda$ :

$$
\left\| f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} \right\| = \left\| f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) - f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*}\right) \right\| \leqslant \left\| J ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} \| \tag {166}
$$

According to Lemma A24, if  $p_{\mathrm{max}} = \max_i p_i \geq 1 - \epsilon$  for all  $\tilde{\pmb{x}} = \lambda \pmb{\xi} + (1 - \lambda) \pmb{x}_i^*$ , then the spectral norm of the Jacobian is bounded by

$$
\left\| \mathrm {J} _ {s} (\tilde {\boldsymbol {x}}) \right\| _ {2} <   2 \epsilon \beta . \tag {167}
$$

The norm of Jacobian at  $\tilde{x}$  is bounded

$$
\left\| \mathrm {J} (\tilde {\boldsymbol {x}}) \right\| _ {2} \leqslant 2 \beta \left\| \boldsymbol {X} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \epsilon \leqslant 2 \beta N M ^ {2} \epsilon . \tag {168}
$$

We used that the spectral norm  $\| .\| _2$  is bounded by the Frobenius norm  $\| .\| _F$  which can be expressed by the norm squared of its column vectors:

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {X} \right\| _ {2} \leqslant \left\| \boldsymbol {X} \right\| _ {F} = \sqrt {\sum_ {i} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| ^ {2}}. \tag {169}
$$

Therefore

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {X} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \leqslant N M ^ {2}. \tag {170}
$$

The norm of Jacobian of the fixed point iteration is bounded

$$
\left\| \mathbf {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \leqslant 2 \beta \left\| \boldsymbol {X} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \epsilon \leqslant 2 \beta N M ^ {2} \epsilon . \tag {171}
$$

The separation of pattern  $\pmb{x}_i$  from data  $\mathbf{X} = (x_{1},\dots,x_{N})$  is

$$
\Delta_ {i} = \min  _ {j, j \neq i} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) = \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \max  _ {j, j \neq i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}. \tag {172}
$$

We need the separation  $\tilde{\Delta}_i$  of  $\tilde{\pmb{x}} = \lambda \pmb {\xi} + (1 - \lambda)\pmb{x}_i^*$  from the data:

$$
\tilde {\Delta} _ {i} = \min  _ {j, j \neq i} \left(\tilde {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \tilde {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right). \tag {173}
$$

We compute a lower bound on  $\tilde{\Delta}_i$ . Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain for  $1 \leqslant j \leqslant N$ :

$$
\left| \tilde {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right| \leqslant \| \tilde {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| \leqslant \| \tilde {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| M. \tag {174}
$$

We have the lower bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tilde {\Delta} _ {i} \geq \min  _ {j, j \neq i} \left(\left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \| \tilde {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| M\right) - \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} + \| \tilde {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| M\right)\right) \tag {175} \\ = - 2 \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| M + \min _ {j, j \neq i} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) = \Delta_ {i} - 2 \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| M. \\ \end{array}
$$

Since

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| = \left\| \lambda \boldsymbol {\xi} + (1 - \lambda) \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| \tag {176} \\ \leqslant \lambda \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| + (1 - \lambda) \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \\ \leqslant \max  \left\{\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \|, \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \right\}, \\ \end{array}
$$

we have

$$
\tilde {\Delta} _ {i} \geq \Delta_ {i} - 2 \max  \left\{\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \|, \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \right\} M. \tag {177}
$$

For the softmax component  $i$  we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} [ \operatorname {s o f t m a x} (\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}}) ] _ {i} = \frac {1}{1 + \sum_ {j \neq i} \exp \left(\beta \left(\tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} - \tilde {\boldsymbol {\xi}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right)\right)} \tag {178} \\ \geq \frac {1}{1 + \sum_ {j \neq i} \exp (- \beta (\Delta_ {i} - 2 \max \{\| \pmb {\xi} - \pmb {x} _ {i} \| , \| \pmb {x} _ {i} ^ {*} - \pmb {x} _ {i} \| \} M))} \\ = \frac {1}{1 + (N - 1) \exp (- \beta (\Delta_ {i} - 2 \max  \{\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| , \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \} M))} \\ = 1 - \frac {(N - 1) \exp (- \beta (\Delta_ {i} - 2 \max  \{\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| , \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \} M))}{1 + (N - 1) \exp (- \beta (\Delta_ {i} - 2 \max  \{\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| , \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \} M))} \\ \geq 1 - (N - 1) \exp (- \beta (\Delta_ {i} - 2 \max  \{\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \|, \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \} M)) \\ = 1 - \epsilon . \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore

$$
\epsilon = (N - 1) \exp (- \beta (\Delta_ {i} - 2 \max  \{\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \|, \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \} M)). \tag {179}
$$

We can bound the spectral norm of the Jacobian, which upper bounds the Lipschitz constant:

$$
\left\| J ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \leqslant 2 \beta N M ^ {2} (N - 1) \exp (- \beta (\Delta_ {i} - 2 \max  \{\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \|, \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \} M)). \tag {180}
$$

For a contraction mapping we require

$$
\left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} <   1, \tag {181}
$$

which can be ensured by

$$
2 \beta N M ^ {2} (N - 1) \exp (- \beta (\Delta_ {i} - 2 \max  \{\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \|, \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \} M)) <   1. \tag {182}
$$

Solving this inequality for  $\Delta_{i}$  gives

$$
\Delta_ {i} > 2 \max  \left\{\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \|, \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \right\} M + \frac {1}{\beta} \ln (2 (N - 1) N \beta M ^ {2}). \tag {183}
$$

In an environment around  $\pmb{x}_i^*$  in which Eq. (183) holds,  $f$  is a contraction mapping and every point converges under the iteration  $f$  to  $\pmb{x}_i^*$  when the iteration stays in the environment. After every iteration the mapped point  $f(\pmb{\xi})$  is closer to the fixed point  $\pmb{x}_i^*$  than the original point  $\pmb{x}_i$ :

$$
\left\| f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} \right\| \leqslant \left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} \right\| <   \left\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} \right\|. \tag {184}
$$

Using

$$
\left\| f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} \right\| \leqslant \left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} \right\| \leqslant \left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) \right\| + \left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2} \left\| f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} \right\|, \tag {185}
$$

we obtain

$$
\left\| f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {*} \right\| \leqslant \frac {\left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2}}{1 - \left\| \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \right\| _ {2}} \left\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) \right\|. \tag {186}
$$

For large  $\Delta_{i}$  the iteration is close to the fixed point even after one update. This has been confirmed in several experiments.

# A.1.5.4 Metastable States: Fixed Points Near Mean of Similar Patterns. The proof concept is the same as for a single pattern but now for the arithmetic mean of similar patterns.

- Bound on the Jacobian.

The Jacobian of the fixed point iteration is

$$
J = \beta \boldsymbol {X} (\operatorname {d i a g} (\boldsymbol {p}) - \boldsymbol {p} \boldsymbol {p} ^ {T}) \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} = \boldsymbol {X} J _ {s} \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T}. \tag {187}
$$

If we consider  $p_i$  as the probability of selecting the vector  $\pmb{x}_i$ , then we can define expectations as  $\mathrm{E}_{\pmb{p}}[f(\pmb{x})] = \sum_{i=1}^{N} p_i f(\pmb{x}_i)$ . In this setting the matrix

$$
\boldsymbol {X} \left(\operatorname {d i a g} (\boldsymbol {p}) - \boldsymbol {p} \boldsymbol {p} ^ {T}\right) \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \tag {188}
$$

is the covariance matrix of data  $\mathbf{X}$  when its vectors are selected according to the probability  $p$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {X} \left(\operatorname {d i a g} (\boldsymbol {p}) - \boldsymbol {p p} ^ {T}\right) \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} = \boldsymbol {X} \operatorname {d i a g} (\boldsymbol {p}) \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} - \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {p p} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} (189) \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} - \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} (190) \\ = \operatorname {E} _ {\boldsymbol {p}} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {T} \right] - \operatorname {E} _ {\boldsymbol {p}} [ \boldsymbol {x} ] \operatorname {E} _ {\boldsymbol {p}} [ \boldsymbol {x} ] ^ {T} = \operatorname {V a r} _ {\boldsymbol {p}} [ \boldsymbol {x} ], (191) \\ \end{array}
$$

therefore we have

$$
J = \beta \operatorname {V a r} _ {\boldsymbol {p}} [ \boldsymbol {x} ]. \tag {192}
$$

We now elaborate more on this interpretation as variance. Specifically the singular values of J (or in other words: the covariance) should be reasonably small. The singular values are the key to ensure convergence of the iteration Eq. (57). Next we present some thoughts.

1. It's clear that the largest eigenvalue of the covariance matrix (equal to the largest singular value) is the variance in the direction of the eigenvector associated with the largest eigenvalue.  
2. Furthermore the variance goes to zero as one  $p_i$  goes to one, since only one pattern is chosen and there is no variance.  
3. The variance is reasonable small if all patterns are chosen with equal probability.  
4. The variance is small if few similar patterns are chosen with high probability. If the patterns are sufficient similar, then the spectral norm of the covariance matrix is smaller than one.

The first three issues have already been addressed. Now we focus on the last one in greater detail. We assume that the first  $l$  patterns are much more probable (and similar to one another) than the other patterns. Therefore, we define:

$$
M := \max  _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \|, \tag {193}
$$

$$
\gamma = \sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \leqslant \epsilon , \tag {194}
$$

$$
1 - \gamma = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \geq 1 - \epsilon , \tag {195}
$$

$$
\tilde {p} _ {i} := \frac {p _ {i}}{1 - \gamma} \leqslant p _ {i} / (1 - \epsilon), \tag {196}
$$

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} \tilde {p} _ {i} = 1, \tag {197}
$$

$$
\boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} = \frac {1}{l} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \tag {198}
$$

$$
m _ {\max } = \max  _ {1 \leqslant i \leqslant l} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \|. \tag {199}
$$

$M$  is an upper bound on the Euclidean norm of the patterns, which are vectors.  $\epsilon$  is an upper bound on the probability  $\gamma$  of not choosing one of the first  $l$  patterns, while  $1 - \epsilon$  is a lower bound the probability  $(1 - \gamma)$  of choosing one of the first  $l$  patterns.  $\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}$  is the arithmetic mean (the center) of the first  $l$  patterns.  $m_{\mathrm{max}}$  is the maximal distance of the patterns to the center  $\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}$ .  $\tilde{\pmb{p}}$  is the probability  $\pmb{p}$  normalized for the first  $l$  patterns.

The variance of the first  $l$  patterns is

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {V a r} _ {\tilde {p}} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} _ {1: l} \right] = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} \tilde {p} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} - \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} \tilde {p} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} \tilde {p} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \tag {200} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} \tilde {p} _ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} \tilde {p} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} \tilde {p} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Lemma A8. With the definitions in Eq. (193) to Eq. (200), the following bounds on the norm  $\| \mathrm{J}\| _2$  of the Jacobian of the fixed point iteration hold. The  $\gamma$ -bound for  $\| \mathbf{J}\| _2$  is

$$
\left\| J \right\| _ {2} \leqslant \beta \left((1 - \gamma) m _ {\max } ^ {2} + \gamma 2 (2 - \gamma) M ^ {2}\right) \tag {201}
$$

and the  $\epsilon$ -bound for  $\| \mathbf{J}\| _2$  is:

$$
\left\| J \right\| _ {2} \leqslant \beta \left(m _ {\max } ^ {2} + \epsilon 2 (2 - \epsilon) M ^ {2}\right). \tag {202}
$$

Proof. The variance  $\mathrm{Var}_{\tilde{p}}[\pmb{x}_{1:l}]$  can be expressed as:

$$
\begin{array}{l} (1 - \gamma) \operatorname {V a r} _ {\tilde {p}} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} _ {1: l} \right] = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \frac {1}{1 - \gamma} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \frac {1}{1 - \gamma} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \tag {203} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} - \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \frac {1}{1 - \gamma} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} - \frac {1}{1 - \gamma} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \\ + \frac {\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i}}{(1 - \gamma) ^ {2}} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} - \frac {1}{1 - \gamma} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} - \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} + \left(1 - \frac {1}{1 - \gamma}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} - \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} - \frac {\gamma}{1 - \gamma} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} - \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \tag {204} \\ = (1 - \gamma) \operatorname {V a r} _ {\tilde {p}} [ \boldsymbol {x} _ {1: l} ] + \frac {\gamma}{1 - \gamma} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T}. \\ \end{array}
$$

We now can reformulate the Jacobian J:

$$
\begin{array}{l} J = \beta \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} + \sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \right. \tag {205} \\ - \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} + \sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} + \sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \\ = \beta \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} - \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \right. \\ + \sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} - \left(\sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \\ - \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} - \left(\sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \\ = \beta \left((1 - \gamma) \operatorname {V a r} _ {\hat {p}} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} _ {1: l} \right] + \frac {\gamma}{1 - \gamma} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \right. \\ + \sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} - \left(\sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \\ - \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} - \left(\sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T}. \\ \end{array}
$$

The spectral norm of an outer product of two vectors is the product of the Euclidean norms of the vectors:

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {a} \boldsymbol {b} ^ {T} \right\| _ {2} = \sqrt {\lambda_ {\max } (\boldsymbol {b} \boldsymbol {a} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {a} \boldsymbol {b} ^ {T})} = \| \boldsymbol {a} \| \sqrt {\lambda_ {\max } (\boldsymbol {b} \boldsymbol {b} ^ {T})} = \| \boldsymbol {a} \| \| \boldsymbol {b} \|, \tag {206}
$$

since  $\pmb{b}\pmb{b}^T$  has eigenvector  $\pmb {b} / \| \pmb {b}\|$  with eigenvalue  $\| \pmb {b}\| ^2$  and otherwise zero eigenvalues.

We now bound the norms of some matrices and vectors:

$$
\left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| \leqslant \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \leqslant (1 - \gamma) M, \tag {207}
$$

$$
\left\| \sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \right\| \leqslant \sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| \leqslant \gamma M, \tag {208}
$$

$$
\left\| \sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \right\| _ {2} \leqslant \sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \right\| _ {2} = \sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| ^ {2} \leqslant \sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} M ^ {2} = \gamma M ^ {2}. \tag {209}
$$

In order to bound the variance of the first  $l$  patterns, we compute the vector  $\pmb{a}$  that minimizes

$$
f (\boldsymbol {a}) = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {a} \| ^ {2} = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {a}\right) ^ {T} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {a}\right). \tag {210}
$$

The solution to

$$
\frac {\partial f (\boldsymbol {a})}{\partial \boldsymbol {a}} = 2 \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} (\boldsymbol {a} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}) = 0 \tag {211}
$$

is

$$
\boldsymbol {a} = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}. \tag {212}
$$

The Hessian of  $f$  is positive definite since

$$
\frac {\partial^ {2} f (\boldsymbol {a})}{\partial \boldsymbol {a} ^ {2}} = 2 \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {I} = 2 \boldsymbol {I} \tag {213}
$$

and  $f$  is a convex function. Hence, the mean

$$
\bar {\boldsymbol {x}} := \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \tag {214}
$$

minimizes  $\sum_{i=1}^{N} p_i \| \pmb{x}_i - \pmb{a} \|^2$ . Therefore, we have

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} \right\| ^ {2} \leqslant \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \right\| ^ {2} \leqslant (1 - \gamma) m _ {\max } ^ {2}. \tag {215}
$$

We now bound the variance on the first  $l$  patterns:

$$
\begin{array}{l} (1 - \gamma) \| \operatorname {V a r} _ {\tilde {p}} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} _ {1: l} \right] \| _ {2} \leqslant \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}}\right) ^ {T} \right\| _ {2} \tag {216} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} \| ^ {2} \leqslant \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \| ^ {2} \leqslant (1 - \gamma) m _ {\max } ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

We obtain for the spectral norm of J:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathrm {J} \right\| _ {2} \leqslant \beta \left((1 - \gamma) \left\| \operatorname {V a r} _ {\tilde {p}} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} _ {1: l} \right] \right\| _ {2} \right. \tag {217} \\ + \frac {\gamma}{1 - \gamma} \left\| \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \right\| _ {2} \\ + \left\| \sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {T} \right\| _ {2} + \left\| \left(\sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \right\| _ {2} \\ + \left\| \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \right\| _ {2} + \left\| \left(\sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right) ^ {T} \right\| _ {2}) \\ \leqslant \beta \left((1 - \gamma) \| \operatorname {V a r} _ {\bar {p}} [ \boldsymbol {x} _ {1: l} ] \| _ {2} + \gamma (1 - \gamma) M ^ {2} + \gamma M ^ {2} + \gamma^ {2} M ^ {2} + \right. \\ \gamma (1 - \gamma) M ^ {2} + \gamma (1 - \gamma) M ^ {2}) \\ = \beta \left((1 - \gamma) \| \mathrm {V a r} _ {\tilde {p}} [ \boldsymbol {x} _ {1: l} ] \| _ {2} + \gamma 2 (2 - \gamma) M ^ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Combining the previous two estimates immediately leads to Eq. (201).

The function  $h(x) = x2(2 - x)$  has the derivative  $h^\prime (x) = 4(1 - x)$ . Therefore,  $h(x)$  is monotone increasing for  $x < 1$ . For  $0\leqslant \gamma \leqslant \epsilon < 1$ , we can immediately deduce that  $\gamma 2(2 - \gamma)\leqslant \epsilon 2(2 - \epsilon)$ . Since  $\epsilon$  is larger than  $\gamma$ , we obtain the following  $\epsilon$ -bound for  $\| \mathrm{J}\| _2$ :

$$
\left\| J \right\| _ {2} \leqslant \beta \left(m _ {\max } ^ {2} + \epsilon 2 (2 - \epsilon) M ^ {2}\right). \tag {218}
$$

![](images/c48f3a61a32b06b32629fbf91ea13c819fa018b62ca56de782ea0a0b822f69b7.jpg)

We revisit the bound on  $(1 - \gamma)\operatorname{Var}_{\tilde{p}}[\boldsymbol{x}_{1:l}]$ . The trace  $\sum_{k=1}^{d}e_k$  is the sum of the eigenvalues  $e_k$ . The spectral norm is equal to the largest eigenvalue  $e_1$ , that is, the largest singular value. We obtain:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \operatorname {V a r} _ {\tilde {p}} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} _ {1: l} \right] \right\| _ {2} = \operatorname {T r} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}}\right) ^ {T}\right) - \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {d} e _ {k} \tag {219} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \operatorname {T r} \left(\left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}}\right) ^ {T}\right) - \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {d} e _ {k} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} \| ^ {2} - \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {d} e _ {k}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore, the tightness of the bound depends on eigenvalues which are not the largest. That is variations which are not along the strongest variation weaken the bound.

Proof of a fixed point by Banach Fixed Point Theorem.

Without restricting the generality, we assume that the first  $l$  patterns are much more probable (and similar to one another) than the other patterns. Therefore, we define:

$$
M := \max  _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \|, \tag {220}
$$

$$
\gamma = \sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \leqslant \epsilon , \tag {221}
$$

$$
1 - \gamma = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \geq 1 - \epsilon , \tag {222}
$$

$$
\tilde {p} _ {i} := \frac {p _ {i}}{1 - \gamma} \leqslant p _ {i} / (1 - \epsilon), \tag {223}
$$

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} \tilde {p} _ {i} = 1, \tag {224}
$$

$$
\boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} = \frac {1}{l} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \tag {225}
$$

$$
m _ {\max } = \max  _ {1 \leqslant i \leqslant l} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \|. \tag {226}
$$

$M$  is an upper bound on the Euclidean norm of the patterns, which are vectors.  $\epsilon$  is an upper bound on the probability  $\gamma$  of not choosing one of the first  $l$  patterns, while  $1 - \epsilon$  is a lower bound the probability  $(1 - \gamma)$  of choosing one of the first  $l$  patterns.  $\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}$  is the arithmetic mean (the center) of the first  $l$  patterns.  $m_{\mathrm{max}}$  is the maximal distance of the patterns to the center  $\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}$ .  $\tilde{\pmb{p}}$  is the probability  $\pmb{p}$  normalized for the first  $l$  patterns.

- Mapped vectors stay in a compact environment. We show that if  $m_{\pmb{x}}$  is sufficient dissimilar to other  $x_{j}$  with  $l < j$  then there is an compact environment of  $m_{\pmb{x}}$  (a sphere) where the fixed point iteration maps this environment into itself. The idea of the proof is to define a sphere around  $m_{\pmb{x}}$  for which the points from the sphere are mapped by  $f$  into the sphere.

We first need following lemma which bounds the distance  $\| m_{\pmb{x}} - f(\pmb{\xi})\|$  of a  $\pmb{\xi}$  which is close to  $m_{x}$ .

Lemma A9. For a query  $\pmb{\xi}$  and data  $\mathbf{X} = (\pmb{x}_1, \dots, \pmb{x}_N)$ , we define

$$
0 \leqslant c = \min  _ {j, l <   j} \left(\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) = \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \max  _ {j, l <   j} \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}. \tag {227}
$$

The following holds:

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) \right\| \leqslant m _ {\max } + 2 \gamma M \leqslant m _ {\max } + 2 \epsilon M, \tag {228}
$$

where

$$
M = \max  _ {i} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \|, \tag {229}
$$

$$
\epsilon = (N - l) \exp (- \beta c). \tag {230}
$$

Proof. Let  $s = \arg \max_{j,j\leqslant l}\pmb{\xi}^T\pmb{x}_j$ , therefore  $\pmb{\xi}^T\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}} = \frac{1}{l}\sum_{i = 1}^{l}\pmb{\xi}^T\pmb{x}_i\leqslant \frac{1}{l}\sum_{i = 1}^{l}\pmb{\xi}^T\pmb{x}_s = \pmb{\xi}^T\pmb{x}_s$ . For softmax components  $j$  with  $l < j$  we have

$$
[ \operatorname {s o f t m a x} (\beta \boldsymbol {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {\xi}) ] _ {j} = \frac {\exp (\beta (\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} - \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {s}))}{1 + \sum_ {k , k \neq s} \exp (\beta (\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {k} - \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {s}))} \leqslant \exp (- \beta c) = \frac {\epsilon}{N - l}, \tag {231}
$$

since  $\pmb{\xi}^T\pmb{x}_s - \pmb{\xi}^T\pmb{x}_j \geq \pmb{\xi}^T\pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}} - \pmb{\xi}^T\pmb{x}_j$  for each  $j$  with  $l < j$ , therefore  $\pmb{\xi}^T\pmb{x}_s - \pmb{\xi}^T\pmb{x}_j \geq c$

The iteration  $f$  can be written as

$$
f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) = \mathbf {X} \operatorname {s o f t m a x} \left(\beta \mathbf {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {\xi}\right) = \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {N} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \left[ \operatorname {s o f t m a x} \left(\beta \mathbf {X} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {\xi}\right) \right] _ {j}. \tag {232}
$$

We set  $p_i = [\mathrm{softmax}(\beta \mathbf{X}^T \boldsymbol{\xi})]_i$ , therefore  $\sum_{i=1}^{l} p_i = 1 - \gamma \geq 1 - \epsilon$  and  $\sum_{i=l+1}^{N} p_i = \gamma \leqslant \epsilon$ . Therefore

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} \frac {p _ {j}}{1 - \gamma} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\| ^ {2} = \left\| \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} \frac {p _ {j}}{1 - \gamma} \left(\boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \tag {233} \\ = \sum_ {j = 1, k = 1} ^ {l} \frac {p _ {j}}{1 - \gamma} \frac {p _ {k}}{1 - \gamma} \left(\boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) ^ {T} \left(\boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {k}\right) \\ = \frac {1}{2} \sum_ {j = 1, k = 1} ^ {l} \frac {p _ {j}}{1 - \gamma} \frac {p _ {k}}{1 - \gamma} \left(\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {k} \| ^ {2}\right) \\ = \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} \frac {p _ {j}}{1 - \gamma} \| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2} \sum_ {j = 1, k = 1} ^ {l} \frac {p _ {j}}{1 - \gamma} \frac {p _ {k}}{1 - \gamma} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \leqslant \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} \frac {p _ {j}}{1 - \gamma} \left\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\| ^ {2} \leqslant m _ {\max } ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

It follows that

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} \frac {p _ {j}}{1 - \gamma} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\| \leqslant m _ {\max } \tag {234}
$$

We now can bound  $\| \pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}} - f(\pmb{\xi})\|$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) \right\| = \left\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {N} p _ {j} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\| \tag {235} \\ = \left\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} p _ {j} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} - \sum_ {j = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {j} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\| \\ = \left\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} \frac {p _ {j}}{1 - \gamma} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} + \frac {\gamma}{1 - \gamma} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} p _ {j} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} - \sum_ {j = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {j} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\| \\ \leqslant \left\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} \frac {p _ {j}}{1 - \gamma} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\| + \frac {\gamma}{1 - \gamma} \left\| \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} p _ {j} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\| + \left\| \sum_ {j = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {j} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\| \\ \leqslant \left\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} \frac {p _ {j}}{1 - \gamma} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\| + \frac {\gamma}{1 - \gamma} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} p _ {j} M + \sum_ {j = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {j} M \\ \leqslant \left\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} \frac {p _ {j}}{1 - \gamma} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\| + 2 \gamma M \\ \leqslant m _ {\max } + 2 \gamma M \leqslant m _ {\max } + 2 \epsilon M, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we applied Eq. (233) in the penultimate inequality. This is the statement of the lemma.

![](images/0b902737b432d3104c21ed47ccc2014ffb5775a3ffa5a2dc466b571813779bab.jpg)

The separation of the center (the arithmetic mean)  $m_{\pmb{x}}$  of the first  $l$  from data  $\mathbf{X} = (\pmb{x}_{l + 1},\dots ,\pmb{x}_N)$  is  $\Delta_{m}$ , defined as

$$
\Delta_ {m} = \min  _ {j, l <   j} \left(\boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) = \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \max  _ {j, l <   j} \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}. \tag {236}
$$

The center is separated from the other data  $\mathbf{x}_j$  with  $l < j$  if  $0 < \Delta_m$ . By the same arguments as in Eq. (140),  $\Delta_m$  can also be expressed as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \Delta_ {m} = \min  _ {j, l <   j} \frac {1}{2} \left(\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \| ^ {2} - \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| ^ {2}\right) \tag {237} \\ = \frac {1}{2} \| \pmb {m} _ {\pmb {x}} \| ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2} \max _ {j, l <   j} \left(\| \pmb {x} _ {j} \| ^ {2} - \| \pmb {m} _ {\pmb {x}} - \pmb {x} _ {j} \| ^ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

For  $\| \pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}}\| = \| \pmb{x}_j\|$  we have  $\Delta_{m} = 1 / 2\min_{j,l < j}\| \pmb{m}_{\pmb{x}} - \pmb{x}_j\|^2$

Next we define the sphere where we want to apply Banach fixed point theorem.

Definition 4 (Sphere  $S_{m}$ ). The sphere  $S_{m}$  is defined as

$$
\mathrm {S} _ {m} := \left\{\boldsymbol {\xi} \mid \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \| \leqslant \frac {1}{\beta m _ {\max }} \right\}. \tag {238}
$$

Lemma A10. With  $\xi$  given, if the assumptions

A1:  $\pmb{\xi}$  is inside sphere:  $\pmb {\xi}\in \mathrm{S}_m$

A2: the center  $m_{\pmb{x}}$  is well separated from other data  $x_{j}$  with  $l < j$ :

$$
\Delta_ {m} \geq \frac {2 M}{\beta m _ {\max }} - \frac {1}{\beta} \ln \left(\frac {1 - \beta m _ {\max } ^ {2}}{2 \beta (N - l) M \max  \left\{m _ {\max } , 2 M \right\}}\right), \tag {239}
$$

A3: the distance  $m_{\mathrm{max}}$  of similar patterns to the center is sufficient small:

$$
\beta m _ {\max } ^ {2} \leqslant 1 \tag {240}
$$

hold, then  $f(\pmb{\xi}) \in \mathrm{S}_m$ . Therefore, under conditions (A2) and (A3),  $f$  is a mapping from  $\mathrm{S}_m$  into  $\mathrm{S}_m$ .

Proof. We need the separation  $\tilde{\Delta}_m$  of  $\pmb{\xi}$  from the rest of the data, which is the last  $N - l$  data points  $\mathbf{X} = (\mathbf{x}_{l + 1},\dots ,\mathbf{x}_N)$ .

$$
\tilde {\Delta} _ {m} = \min  _ {j, l <   j} \left(\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right). \tag {241}
$$

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain for  $l + 1 \leqslant j \leqslant N$ :

$$
\left| \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right| \leqslant \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \| \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| \leqslant \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \| M. \tag {242}
$$

We have the lower bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tilde {\Delta} _ {m} \geq \min  _ {j, l <   j} \left(\left(m _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {T} m _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - m _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \| M\right) - \left(m _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {T} x _ {j} + \| \boldsymbol {\xi} - m _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \| M\right)\right) \tag {243} \\ = - 2 \left\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \right\| M + \min  _ {j, l <   j} \left(\boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {T} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) = \Delta_ {m} - 2 \left\| \boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \right\| M \\ \geq \Delta_ {m} - 2 \frac {M}{\beta m _ {\max }}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used the assumption (A1) of the lemma.

From the proof in Lemma A9 we have

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {l} p _ {i} \geq 1 - (N - l) \exp (- \beta \tilde {\Delta} _ {m}) = 1 - \tilde {\epsilon}, \tag {244}
$$

$$
\sum_ {i = l + 1} ^ {N} p _ {i} \leqslant (N - l) \exp (- \beta \tilde {\Delta} _ {m}) = \tilde {\epsilon}. \tag {245}
$$

Lemma A9 states that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) \right\| \leqslant m _ {\max } + 2 \tilde {\epsilon} M \tag {246} \\ \leqslant m _ {\max } + 2 (N - l) \exp (- \beta \tilde {\Delta} _ {m}) M. \\ \leqslant m _ {\max } + 2 (N - l) \exp (- \beta (\Delta_ {m} - 2 \frac {M}{\beta m _ {\max }})) M. \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} - f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) \right\| \leqslant m _ {\max } + 2 (N - l) \exp \left(- \beta \left(\Delta_ {m} - 2 \frac {M}{\beta m _ {\max }}\right)\right) M \tag {247} \\ \leqslant m _ {\max } + 2 (N - l) \exp \left(- \beta \left(\frac {2 M}{\beta m _ {\max }} - \right. \right. \\ \frac {1}{\beta} \ln \left(\frac {1 - \beta m _ {\max } ^ {2}}{2 \beta (N - l) M \max  \{m _ {\max } , 2 M \}}\right) - 2 \left. \frac {M}{\beta m _ {\max }}\right)\left. \right) M \\ = m _ {\mathrm {m a x}} + 2 (N - l) \frac {1 - \beta m _ {\mathrm {m a x}} ^ {2}}{2 \beta (N - l) M \max \{m _ {\mathrm {m a x}} , 2 M \}} M \\ \leqslant m _ {\mathrm {m a x}} + \frac {1 - \beta m _ {\mathrm {m a x}} ^ {2}}{\beta m _ {\mathrm {m a x}}} = \frac {1}{\beta m _ {\mathrm {m a x}}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used assumption (A2) of the lemma. Therefore,  $f(\pmb{\xi})$  is a mapping from the sphere  $\mathbf{S}_m$  into the sphere  $\mathbf{S}_m$ .

$$
\begin{array}{l} m _ {\max } = \max  _ {1 \leqslant i \leqslant l} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \| (248) \\ = \max  _ {1 \leqslant i \leqslant l} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - 1 / l \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\| (249) \\ = \max  _ {1 \leqslant i \leqslant l} \left\| 1 / l \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {l} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) \right\| (250) \\ \leqslant \max  _ {1 \leqslant i, j \leqslant l} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \| (251) \\ \leqslant \max  _ {1 \leqslant i \leqslant l} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| + \max  _ {1 \leqslant j \leqslant l} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \| (252) \\ \leqslant 2 M (253) \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/6ca653aeeba8f331fa61054e94248a6d11ed95b4c58f64d536ed31d50153bba3.jpg)

- Contraction mapping.

For applying Banach fixed point theorem we need to show that  $f$  is contraction in the compact environment  $S_{m}$ .

Lemma A11. Assume that

A1:

$$
\Delta_ {m} \geq \frac {2 M}{\beta m _ {\operatorname* {m a x}}} - \frac {1}{\beta} \ln \left(\frac {1 - \beta m _ {\operatorname* {m a x}} ^ {2}}{2 \beta (N - l) M \max  \{m _ {\operatorname* {m a x}} , 2 M \}}\right), \tag {254}
$$

and

A2:

$$
\beta m _ {\max } ^ {2} \leqslant 1, \tag {255}
$$

then  $f$  is a contraction mapping in  $S_{m}$ .

Proof. The version of the mean value theorem Lemma A32 states for the symmetric  $\mathrm{J}^m = \int_0^1\mathrm{J}(\lambda \pmb {\xi} + (1 - \lambda)\pmb {m}_x)$  dλ:

$$
f (\boldsymbol {\xi}) = f \left(\boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}}\right) + \mathrm {J} ^ {m} \left(\boldsymbol {\xi} - \boldsymbol {m} _ {\boldsymbol {x}}\right). \tag {256}
$$

In complete analogy to Lemma A6, we get:

$$
